PREFACE 

As in past directories, alumni are listed herein by present geographical location, by classes, and alphabetically. 

All those who have successfully completed two or more years of study at the University of Minnesota Law School are listed herein so far as our information goes. Listings include not only those members who received the 

LL.B. with or without the B.S.L., but also those members who qualified only for the B.S.L.

Business addresses are used in preference to residence addresses. 

The status of alumni as members of the Law Alumni Association was altered in the revised Articles of Incorporation and By Laws adopted by the Association at its annual meeting April 19, 1949. The following excerpts from the revised Articles and By Laws indicate that status as of the present time: 

ARTICLES OF INCORPORATION "Article II. 

"All graduates of the law school of the University of Minnesota and any former student who has earned at least one year of credit in said law school shall be members of this corporation. "Members shall be of four classes, viz.: Life, active, inactive and honorary. "Any member may become a life member upon the payment of the life membership fee of $100.00, after which he shall be relieved of the payment of all dues. "Members whose dues are not in arrears shall be active members. Members whose dues are in arrears for such time as may be provided in the by-laws shall be inactive members. Honorary members shall be such persons as may be so specified or provided for in the by-laws. "The annual dues of members (other than honorary members) shall be as provided in the by-laws but not to exceed $5.00 per year. Honorary members shall be exempt from the payment of dues. "Each life, active and honorary member shall be entitled to one vote at any corporate meeting. Inactive members shall have no power to vote at such meetings." "Members and dues.

BY-LAWS "Section 2. 

"(a) The annual dues of members (other than honorary or life members) shall be in the amount of $2.00 per year and shall accrue and be billed on a calendar year basis, the bills therefor to be mailed to the members as soon after January 1 of each year as is practicable. "(b) Members whose dues are not in arrears shall be active members. "(c) Life and honorary members shall be exempt from the payment of dues. The following persons shall constitute honorary members, viz.: The acting President of the University of Minnesota, the Dean and all members of the faculty of the Law School of the University of Minnesota in active service, all Judges of the United States Courts residing in the State of Minnesota, and all Judges of the Supreme Court and District Courts of the State of Minnesota. Other honorary members may be selected from time to time by the Board of Directors. "(d) Members whose dues are in arrears for two years or more shall be inactive members. Any inactive member may become an active member by the payment of current dues and such part or all of his dues in arrears as the Board of Directors may determine. "(e) Life Membership fees shall be kept in a special separate fund and shall not be used for regular or ordinary expenses of the corporation, but shall be used for such special worthy projects or purposes as may be determined from time to time by the Board of Directors."

VI Every effort has been made to contact all living alumni and to verify information concerning those who have passed on. But it is probably too much to hope that our information is entirely complete and accurate. 

We have received many letters from alumni concerning the whereabouts of those in our "Address Unknown" file and we wish to acknowledge gratefully the assistance so rendered. 

This directory was prepared by Geraldine Shephard with assistance from the law school staff and under the supervision of the Association's treasurer, Professor Stanley V. Kinyon. We sincerely acknowledge and appreciate the many hours of conscientious effort which Mrs. Shephard has devoted to the compilation and verification of data herein. Acknowledgment should also be made of the invaluable records made over the years by Miss Millicent Purdy who prepared several previous editions of the directory. 

THEODORE CHRISTIANSON, JR. 

ROBERT GILLESPIE HENRY SOMSEN, Jr.

VII MESSAGE TO THE ALUMNI 

An examination of this directory will disclose the many parts of the world to which the graduates of this school have gone. Wherever they have gone and in whatever capacity they have served, they have given a good account of themselves.

A brief account of developments pertaining to the school during the past six years, since publication of the last directory, may be of interest. The hard core of the four year Minnesota plan has been retained but some changes in curriculum have been made. The offerings in taxation are being expanded by the addition of a special course in income taxation. A number of seminars have been added and one seminar is required for graduation of each four year student. The courses in Judicial Remedies II and III replace the former courses of Equity, Damages and Restitution. The content of these former courses is taught together so as to bring out the inter-relationship of the various alternative remedies.

During the war, a student entering with a degree was permitted to receive the Bachelor of Laws degree in three years. This has now been extended to three and one half years.

The Supreme Court has adopted some measures which will have an important bearing on legal education. Some of the essential courses of the four year program which had not been on the bar examination list of subjects have been added including as electives Judicial Administration, Legislation, Labor Law, and Creditor's Remedies. An important development was the rule recently adopted by the Minnesota Supreme Court requiring at least six years of education for admission to the bar. Three years of pre-legal work are required before the study of law on a three year full time or four year part time basis. If four years of law study are required by the school, as in the case of this school, students may be admitted after two years of pre-legal education. 

This rule incorporates the standards recently adopted by the American Bar Association.

Alumni of the school will be pleased to know that the Minnesota legislature recognized the space problem of the school and appropriated $600,000 for an addition to the present building. With the law library having reached the 200,000 mark, this addition is greatly needed. The appropriation is also an expression of confidence in the school which is deeply appreciated by every member of its staff.

During the past five years there have been a number of changes in its faculty. The present members are listed on page 1 of this directory. Throughout these changes, the aim has been continuously to maintain the high standards which have characterized the school in the past. This has not always been easy in the face of higher salary levels of competing institutions. 

Student attendance has dropped from a high of over 800 in the fall of 1948 to 360 in the fall of 1952 and 401 in the fall of 1953. This decline was greater than in other law schools of the state. It is believed that larger enrollments are in prospect in the future.

VIII 

In the performance of its duties, the school needs and welcomes the interest and aid of its alumni. The Board of Directors of the Law Alumni Association has been most helpful. Each year a report of the work of the school is made at a special meeting of the Board attended also by all members of the faculty, and at which members of the Board have an opportunity to make suggestions and criticisms. The excellent assistance of the Board and especially of one of its members, Senator A. R. Johanson, Class of 1924, was a vital factor in securing the appropriation for the law school addition. 

The creation of the Wilbur H. Cherry Memorial Scholarship Fund, now totaling over $55,000.00 has improved very greatly the ability of the school to keep those of high scholastic attainment who are in financial need. 

The school and the members of its faculty want your constructive suggestions and criticisms. Only in this way can the school continue to serve its function most effectively.

MAYNARD E. PIRSIG Dean
